=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
Find one shortest palindrome that has S as its prefix.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
S

Output

Print the answer.
If multiple solutions exist, any of them is accepted.

Constraints


- S is a string of length between 1 and 500000, inclusive, consisting of uppercase English letters.

Sample Input 1

ABC

Sample Output 1

ABCBA

ABCBA is a shortest palindrome that has S= ABC as its prefix.

Sample Input 2

Z

Sample Output 2

Z

Z is a shortest palindrome that has S= Z as its prefix.

Sample Input 3

TREE

Sample Output 3

TREERT

TREERT is a shortest palindrome that has S= TREE as its prefix.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Now, implement prefix function.

Pseudo:

def prefix_function(s):
    n = len(s)
    pi = [0]*n
    for i in range(1,n):
        j = pi[i-1]
        while j > 0 and s[i] != s[j]:
            j = pi[j-1]
        if s[i] == s[j]:
            j += 1
        pi[i] = j
    return pi

Now, implementation details.

Potential issue: Prefix function on U = R + '#' + S: We only need pi at end, but to compute pi for S part, we need pi values for R part and separator. Could compute full pi. But maybe we can compute prefix function for R, then continue with '#' and S. Need pi array for fallback. Full pi length 2n+1. Fine.

Could optimize memory by using `array('I')` from array module? But list faster. AtCoder memory maybe 1024 MB. Use list.

Let's write code:

import sys

def main():
    S = sys.stdin.readline().strip()
    n = len(S)
    if n == 1:
        print(S)
        return
    R = S[::-1]
    T = R + '#' + S
    m_total = len(T)
    pi = [0] * m_total
    # compute prefix function
    for i in range(1, m_total):
        j = pi[i-1]
        ti = T[i]
        while j > 0 and ti != T[j]:
            j = pi[j-1]
        if ti == T[j]:
            j += 1
        pi[i] = j
    l = pi[-1]
    m = n - l
    if m == 0:
        print(S)
    else:
        ans = S + S[m-1::-1]
        print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Need check T[j] when j=0 and ti != T[0]? In prefix function, after while, if ti == T[j], j +=1. If j=0, T[0] exists. Good. But if j can equal i? No, j=pi[i-1] <= i-1, so T[j] valid. After while j decreases. Good.

Potential issue: T[j] when j maybe equals m_total? No.

Let's maybe optimize prefix function loop by binding local variables:

def prefix_function(s):
    pi = [0] * len(s)
    j = 0
    for i in range(1, len(s)):
        while j > 0 and s[i] != s[j]:
            j = pi[j-1]
        if s[i] == s[j]:
            j += 1
        pi[i] = j
    return pi

This is standard. We can avoid `j = pi[i-1]` each iteration? Actually j should equal pi[i-1] at start. In the loop, if we maintain j from previous iteration, after setting pi[i]=j, j is pi[i]. At next iteration, j=pi[i] = pi[i-1]. So we don't need assign j=pi[i-1] each time. But careful: In standard algorithm, j is pi[i-1] at start of iteration. After processing, j becomes pi[i]. So we can maintain j across iterations. This is slightly faster. Let's write:

This works because at start of loop i, j = pi[i-1]. Initially i=1, j=pi[0]=0. After iteration, j=pi[i]. Good. Need ensure when i increments, j remains pi[i], so yes. This avoids pi[i-1] lookup. Use local variables for speed:

t = T
p = pi

        j += 1
    p[i] = j

This is efficient. But note `while j and c != t[j]` uses `j` truthiness. If j=0, no t[0] comparison? Then after while, if c == t[0], j becomes 1. Good. If j=0 and c != t[0], j remains 0. Good.

Let's write final code:

import sys

def main():
    S = sys.stdin.readline().strip()
    n = len(S)
    T = S[::-1] + '#' + S
    pi = [0] * len(T)
    j = 0
    for i in range(1, len(T)):
        c = T[i]
        while j > 0 and c != T[j]:
            j = pi[j - 1]
        if c == T[j]:
            j += 1
        pi[i] = j

    l = pi[-1]
    m = n - l
    if m:
        ans = S + S[m-1::-1]
    else:
        ans = S
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Code:

def main():
    import sys
    S = sys.stdin.readline().strip()
    n = len(S)
    T = S[::-1] + '#' + S
    total = len(T)
    pi = [0] * total
    j = 0
    for i in range(1, total):
        c = T[i]
        while j and c != T[j]:
            j = pi[j - 1]
        if c == T[j]:
            j += 1
        pi[i] = j
    m = n - pi[-1]
    if m:
        sys.stdout.write(S + S[m-1::-1] + '\n')
    else:
        sys.stdout.write(S + '\n')

Using `sys.stdout.write` avoids extra space? print okay. Use sys.stdout.write for speed. Need add newline. Good.

Let's write code with bytes:

import sys

def main():
    S = sys.stdin.buffer.readline().strip()
    n = len(S)
    T = S[::-1] + b'#' + S
    total = len(T)
    pi = [0] * total
    j = 0
    for i in range(1, total):
        c = T[i]
        while j and c != T[j]:
            j = pi[j - 1]
        if c == T[j]:
            j += 1
        pi[i] = j
    m = n - pi[-1]
    if m:
        ans = S + S[m-1::-1]
    else:
        ans = S
    sys.stdout.buffer.write(ans + b'\n')

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

This should work. Let's test with sample mentally:

    sys.stdout.buffer.write(ans)
